\def\version{0.3.11}
\def\numberofcommands{81}
\def\numberoftraits{14}
\def\numberoffilehandlers{30}
\def\ebicommands{
\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi analyse all-traces}}
\label{command:Ebi analyse all-traces}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi ana all}.\\
List all traces of a stohastic model. Models containing loops and unbounded models are not supported and the computation will run forever.\\
\noindent Output: finite stochastic language, which can be written as a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}), a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}), a finite stochastic language (.slang -- \cref{filehandler:finite stochastic language}), a stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton}) or a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton}).\\
\\This command is not available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function analyse\_all\_traces.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE}>&Any object with deterministic stochastic semantics.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)} and \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}.\\
\texttt{-t} or \texttt{--output\_type} <\texttt{OUTPUT\_TYPE}> &
The output file extension (without period). The default is slang. Possible values are dfa, lang, slang, sdfa and snfa.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The file to which the results must be written. Based on the file extension, Ebi will output either a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}), a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}), a finite stochastic language (.slang -- \cref{filehandler:finite stochastic language}), a stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton}) or a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton}).
If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT as a finite stochastic language (.slang).\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-a} or \texttt{--approximate} & Use approximate arithmetic instead of exact arithmetic.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_activity} & The name or number of the column that contains the activity.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `1'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header. 
    With this flag set, the first line is read as an event. In the latter case, the columns are given numbers, starting from 0.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. \\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_quote\_character} & The character(s) that begin and end a quote.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `"'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_separator} & The character(s) that separate the columns.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `,'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_trace\_id} & The name or number of the column that contains the trace id.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_ocel\_case\_object\_type} & The object type that defines what a trace is. May be given as the name of the object type (which takes preference) or as the index of the object-type in the order of declaration.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_xes\_event\_classifier} & The attribute that defines, for each event, what its activity is.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `concept:name'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
A \textdagger{} indicates that the file type is converted on import, but that this conversion may fail.
\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi analyse completeness}}
\label{command:Ebi analyse completeness}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi ana comp}.\\
Estimate the completeness of an event log using species discovery.\\
More information: ~\cite{DBLP:conf/icpm/KabierskiRW23}~\\
\noindent Output: fraction, which can be written as a fraction.\\
\\This command is available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function analyse\_completeness.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE}>&An event log.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)} and \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}.\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The fraction file to which the result must be written. If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-a} or \texttt{--approximate} & Use approximate arithmetic instead of exact arithmetic.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_activity} & The name or number of the column that contains the activity.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `1'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header. 
    With this flag set, the first line is read as an event. In the latter case, the columns are given numbers, starting from 0.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. \\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_quote\_character} & The character(s) that begin and end a quote.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `"'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_separator} & The character(s) that separate the columns.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `,'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_trace\_id} & The name or number of the column that contains the trace id.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_ocel\_case\_object\_type} & The object type that defines what a trace is. May be given as the name of the object type (which takes preference) or as the index of the object-type in the order of declaration.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_xes\_event\_classifier} & The attribute that defines, for each event, what its activity is.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `concept:name'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
A \textdagger{} indicates that the file type is converted on import, but that this conversion may fail.
\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi analyse coverage}}
\label{command:Ebi analyse coverage}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi ana cov}.\\
Find the most-likely traces that together cover the given minimum probability.
Will return a finite stochastic language with the extracted traces.
The computation may not terminate if the model has non-decreasing livelocks, or if the model is unbounded and this unboundedness can be triggered using silent transitions, or if the model has livelocks.\\
\noindent Output: finite stochastic language, which can be written as a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}), a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}), a finite stochastic language (.slang -- \cref{filehandler:finite stochastic language}), a stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton}) or a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton}).\\
\\This command is not available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function analyse\_coverage.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE}>&Any object with deterministic stochastic semantics.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)} and \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}.\\
<\texttt{MINIMUM\_COVERAGE}>&The minimum probability that a trace should have to be included.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad fraction between 0 and 1.\\
\texttt{-t} or \texttt{--output\_type} <\texttt{OUTPUT\_TYPE}> &
The output file extension (without period). The default is slang. Possible values are dfa, lang, slang, sdfa and snfa.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The file to which the results must be written. Based on the file extension, Ebi will output either a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}), a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}), a finite stochastic language (.slang -- \cref{filehandler:finite stochastic language}), a stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton}) or a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton}).
If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT as a finite stochastic language (.slang).\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-a} or \texttt{--approximate} & Use approximate arithmetic instead of exact arithmetic.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_activity} & The name or number of the column that contains the activity.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `1'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header. 
    With this flag set, the first line is read as an event. In the latter case, the columns are given numbers, starting from 0.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. \\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_quote\_character} & The character(s) that begin and end a quote.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `"'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_separator} & The character(s) that separate the columns.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `,'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_trace\_id} & The name or number of the column that contains the trace id.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_ocel\_case\_object\_type} & The object type that defines what a trace is. May be given as the name of the object type (which takes preference) or as the index of the object-type in the order of declaration.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_xes\_event\_classifier} & The attribute that defines, for each event, what its activity is.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `concept:name'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
A \textdagger{} indicates that the file type is converted on import, but that this conversion may fail.

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi analyse medoid}}
\label{command:Ebi analyse medoid}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi ana med}.\\
Find the traces with the lowest average normalised Levenshtein distance to the other traces.
If there are more than one such trace, an arbitrary one is returned.\\
\noindent Output: finite language, which can be written as a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}) or a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}).\\
\\This command is not available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function analyse\_medoid.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE}>&Any object with a finite stochastic language.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)} and \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}.\\
<\texttt{NUMBER\_OF\_TRACES}>&The number of traces that should be extracted.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no: if no value is provided, a default of 1 will be used. It can also be provided on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad integer.\\
\texttt{-t} or \texttt{--output\_type} <\texttt{OUTPUT\_TYPE}> &
The output file extension (without period). The default is lang. Possible values are dfa and lang.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The file to which the results must be written. Based on the file extension, Ebi will output either a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}) or a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}).
If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT as a finite language (.lang).\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-a} or \texttt{--approximate} & Use approximate arithmetic instead of exact arithmetic.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_activity} & The name or number of the column that contains the activity.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `1'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header. 
    With this flag set, the first line is read as an event. In the latter case, the columns are given numbers, starting from 0.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. \\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_quote\_character} & The character(s) that begin and end a quote.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `"'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_separator} & The character(s) that separate the columns.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `,'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_trace\_id} & The name or number of the column that contains the trace id.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_ocel\_case\_object\_type} & The object type that defines what a trace is. May be given as the name of the object type (which takes preference) or as the index of the object-type in the order of declaration.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_xes\_event\_classifier} & The attribute that defines, for each event, what its activity is.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `concept:name'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\

\end{tabularx}

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi analyse minimum-probability-traces}}
\label{command:Ebi analyse minimum-probability-traces}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi ana minprob}.\\
Find all traces that have a given minimum probability.
Will return a finate stochastic language with the extracted traces.
Will return an error if there are no such traces.
The computation may not terminate if the model is unbounded and this unboundedness can be triggered using silent transitions.\\
\noindent Output: finite stochastic language, which can be written as a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}), a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}), a finite stochastic language (.slang -- \cref{filehandler:finite stochastic language}), a stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton}) or a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton}).\\
\\This command is not available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function analyse\_minimum\_probability\_traces.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE}>&Any object with deterministic stochastic semantics.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)} and \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}.\\
<\texttt{MINIMUM\_PROBABILITY}>&The minimum probability that a trace should have to be included.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad fraction between 0 and 1.\\
\texttt{-t} or \texttt{--output\_type} <\texttt{OUTPUT\_TYPE}> &
The output file extension (without period). The default is slang. Possible values are dfa, lang, slang, sdfa and snfa.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The file to which the results must be written. Based on the file extension, Ebi will output either a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}), a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}), a finite stochastic language (.slang -- \cref{filehandler:finite stochastic language}), a stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton}) or a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton}).
If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT as a finite stochastic language (.slang).\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-a} or \texttt{--approximate} & Use approximate arithmetic instead of exact arithmetic.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_activity} & The name or number of the column that contains the activity.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `1'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header. 
    With this flag set, the first line is read as an event. In the latter case, the columns are given numbers, starting from 0.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. \\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_quote\_character} & The character(s) that begin and end a quote.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `"'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_separator} & The character(s) that separate the columns.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `,'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_trace\_id} & The name or number of the column that contains the trace id.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_ocel\_case\_object\_type} & The object type that defines what a trace is. May be given as the name of the object type (which takes preference) or as the index of the object-type in the order of declaration.. &\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi analyse mode}}
\label{command:Ebi analyse mode}
Find the trace with the highest probability.
If there is more than one trace with the highest probability, an arbitrary choice is made which one to return.
The computation may run forever if the model is unbounded.
Equivalent to `Ebi evaluate mostlikely 1`.
Computation is more efficient for a model with a finite stochastic language.\\
\noindent Output: finite stochastic language, which can be written as a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}), a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}), a finite stochastic language (.slang -- \cref{filehandler:finite stochastic language}), a stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton}) or a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton}).\\
\\This command is not available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function analyse\_mode.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE}>&Any object with deterministic stochastic semantics.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)} and \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)}.\\
\texttt{-t} or \texttt{--output\_type} <\texttt{OUTPUT\_TYPE}> &
The output file extension (without period). The default is slang. Possible values are dfa, lang, slang, sdfa and snfa.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The file to which the results must be written. Based on the file extension, Ebi will output either a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}), a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}), a finite stochastic language (.slang -- \cref{filehandler:finite stochastic language}), a stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton}) or a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton}).
If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT as a finite stochastic language (.slang).\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-a} or \texttt{--approximate} & Use approximate arithmetic instead of exact arithmetic.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_activity} & The name or number of the column that contains the activity.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `1'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header. 
    With this flag set, the first line is read as an event. In the latter case, the columns are given numbers, starting from 0.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. \\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_quote\_character} & The character(s) that begin and end a quote.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `"'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_separator} & The character(s) that separate the columns.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `,'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_trace\_id} & The name or number of the column that contains the trace id.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_ocel\_case\_object\_type} & The object type that defines what a trace is. May be given as the name of the object type (which takes preference) or as the index of the object-type in the order of declaration.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_xes\_event\_classifier} & The attribute that defines, for each event, what its activity is.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `concept:name'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\

\end{tabularx}

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi analyse most-likely-traces}}
\label{command:Ebi analyse most-likely-traces}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi ana mostlikely}.\\
Find the given number of traces with the highest probabilities.
If there are more than one trace with the same probability, an arbitrary choice is made which one to return.
The computation may run forever if the model is unbounded.
Computation is more efficient for an object with a finite stochastic language.\\
\noindent Output: finite stochastic language, which can be written as a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}), a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}), a finite stochastic language (.slang -- \cref{filehandler:finite stochastic language}), a stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton}) or a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton}).\\
\\This command is not available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function analyse\_most\_likely\_traces.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE}>&Any object with deterministic stochastic semantics.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)} and \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}.\\
<\texttt{NUMBER\_OF\_TRACES}>&The number of traces that should be extracted.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad integer above 1.\\
\texttt{-t} or \texttt{--output\_type} <\texttt{OUTPUT\_TYPE}> &

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi analyse-non-stochastic any-traces}}
\label{command:Ebi analyse-non-stochastic any-traces}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi anans at}.\\
Compute whether the model has any traces.
        Reasons for a model not to have any traces could be if the initial state is part of a livelock, or if there is no initial state.
        'true' means that the model has traces, 'false' means that the model has no traces.
        The computation may not terminate if the model is unbounded.\\
\noindent Output: bool, which can be written as a boolean.\\
\\This command is available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function analyse\_non\_stochastic\_any\_traces.

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi analyse-non-stochastic cluster}}
\label{command:Ebi analyse-non-stochastic cluster}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi anans clus}.\\
Apply k-medoid clustering: group the traces into a given number of clusters, such that the average distance of each trace to its closest medoid is minimal. The computation is random and does not take into account how often each trace occurs.\\
More information: ~\cite{DBLP:journals/is/SchubertR21}~\\
\noindent Output: finite language, which can be written as a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}) or a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}).\\
\\This command is not available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function analyse\_non\_stochastic\_cluster.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{LANGUAGE}>&The finite stochastic language.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite language]{finite language (.lang)} and \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}.\\
<\texttt{NUMBER\_OF\_CLUSTERS}>&The number of clusters.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad integer above 1.\\
\texttt{-t} or \texttt{--output\_type} <\texttt{OUTPUT\_TYPE}> &
The output file extension (without period). The default is lang. Possible values are dfa and lang.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The file to which the results must be written. Based on the file extension, Ebi will output either a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}) or a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}).
If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT as a finite language (.lang).\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi analyse-non-stochastic medoid}}
\label{command:Ebi analyse-non-stochastic medoid}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi anans med}.\\
Find the traces with the lowest average normalised Levenshtein distance to the other traces; ties are resolved arbritrarily. The computation is random and does not take into account how often each trace occurs.\\
\noindent Output: finite language, which can be written as a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}) or a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}).\\

\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function analyse\_non\_stochastic\_medoid.

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi conformance chi-squared}}
\label{command:Ebi conformance chi-squared}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi conf cssc}.\\
Compute chi-square stochastic conformance.\\
More information: Chi-squared stochastic conformance is computed as follows:
        
        \begin{definition}[Chi-squared stochastic conformance~\cite{DBLP:conf/eccv/PeleW10}]
            \label{def:cssc}
                Let $L$ be a finite stochastic language and let $M$ be a queriable stochastic langauge.
                Then, the \emph{chi-squared stochastic conformance ($\text{cssc}$) of $L$ and $M$} is defined as follows: 
                \begin{align*}
                    \text{cssc}(L, M) ={}& 1 - \frac{1}{2} \sum_{\sigma \in L \cup M} \frac{(L(\sigma)-M(\sigma))^2}{L(\sigma)+M(\sigma)}
                \end{align*}       
            \end{definition}~\\
\noindent Output: fraction, which can be written as a fraction.\\
\\This command is available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function conformance\_chi\_squared.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE\_1}>&A finite stochastic language (log) to compare.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)} and \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}.\\
<\texttt{FILE\_2}>&A queriable stochastic language (model) to compare.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)} and \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}.\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The fraction file to which the result must be written. If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-a} or \texttt{--approximate} & Use approximate arithmetic instead of exact arithmetic.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_activity} & The name or number of the column that contains the activity.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE\_1. The default value is `1'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header. 
    With this flag set, the first line is read as an event. In the latter case, the columns are given numbers, starting from 0.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE\_1. \\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_quote\_character} & The character(s) that begin and end a quote.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE\_1. The default value is `"'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_separator} & The character(s) that separate the columns.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE\_1. The default value is `,'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_trace\_id} & The name or number of the column that contains the trace id.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE\_1. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi conformance earth-movers}}
\label{command:Ebi conformance earth-movers}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi conf emsc}.\\
Compute Earth mover's stochastic conformance, which is 1 - the Wasserstein distance.\\
More information: Earth movers' stochastic conformance is computed as follows:
        
        \begin{definition}[Earth movers' stochastic conformance~\cite{DBLP:journals/is/LeemansABP21}]
            \label{def:emsc}
                Let $L$ and $L'$ be a finite stochastic languages.
                Then, the \emph{Earth movers' stochastic conformance ($\text{emsc}$) of $L$ and $L'$} is defined as follows, with reallocation matrix $R$ and normalised Levenshtein distance $\delta$:
                \begin{align*}
                    \text{emsc}(L, L') ={}& 1 - \min_{R} \sum_{t \in L}\sum_{t' \in L'} R(t, t') \delta(t,t')\\
                    \text{ such that } & \forall_{t\in L} L(t) \leq \sum_{t' \in L'} R(t, t') \land{}\\
                    & \forall_{t' \in L'} L'(t') \leq \sum_{t \in L} R(t, t') \land{}\\
                    & \sum_{t \in L} \sum_{t' \in L'} R(t,t') = 1
                \end{align*}       
            \end{definition}
            
        In case one or both of the inputs are partially ordered, EMSC is computed with a corresponding distance function.~\\
\noindent Output: fraction, which can be written as a fraction.\\
\\This command is available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function conformance\_earth\_movers.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE\_1}>&A finite stochastic language to compare (may be partially ordered).\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic partially ordered language]{finite stochastic partially ordered language (.spolang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)} and \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}.\\

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi conformance entropic-relevance}}
\label{command:Ebi conformance entropic-relevance}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi conf er}.\\
Compute entropic relevance (uniform).\\
More information: Entropic relevance is computed as follows:
        
        \begin{definition}[Entropic Relevance~\cite{DBLP:journals/is/AlkhammashPMG22}]
            \label{def:ER}
                Let $L$ be a finite stochastic language and let $M$ be a queriable stochastic langauge.
                Let $\Lambda$ be the set of all activities appearing in the traces of $L$.
                Then, the \emph{entropic relevance ($\entrel$) of $M$ to $L$} is defined as follows: 
                \begin{align*}
                    \entrel(L, M) ={}& H_0\left(\sum_{\sigma \in \bar{L},\, M(\sigma)>0}{L(\sigma)}\right) + 
                    \sum_{\sigma \in \bar{L}}L(\sigma) J(\sigma, M)\\
                    J(\sigma, M) ={}& \begin{cases}
                    -\log_2 M(\sigma) & M(\sigma) > 0\\
                    (1+|\sigma|) \log_2 (1 + |\Lambda|)) & \text{otherwise}
                    \end{cases}\\
                    H_0(x) ={}& -x \log_2{x} - (1-x) \log_2{(1-x)} \text{ with } H_0(0) = H_0(1) = 0 &\\
                \end{align*}       
            \end{definition}~\\
\noindent Output: logarithm, which can be written as a logarithm.\\
\\This command is available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function conformance\_entropic\_relevance.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE\_1}>&A finite stochastic language (log) to compare.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)} and \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}.\\
<\texttt{FILE\_2}>&A queriable stochastic language (model) to compare.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)} and \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}.\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi conformance hellinger}}
\label{command:Ebi conformance hellinger}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi conf hsc}.\\
Compute Hellinger stochastic conformance, which is 1 - the Hellinger distance.\\
More information: Hellinger stochastic conformance is computed as follows:
        
        \begin{definition}[Hellinger stochastic conformance~\cite{hellinger1909neue}]
            \label{def:hsc}
                Let $L$ be a finite stochastic language and let $M$ be a queriable stochastic langauge.
                Then, the \emph{Hellinger stochastic conformance ($\text{hsc}$) of $L$ and $M$} is defined as follows: 
                \begin{align*}
                    \text{hsc}(L, M) ={}& \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{\sigma \in L \cup M}(\sqrt{L(x)}-\sqrt{M(x)})^2}
                \end{align*}       
            \end{definition}~\\
\noindent Output: fraction, which can be written as a fraction.\\
\\This command is available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function conformance\_hellinger.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE\_1}>&A finite stochastic language (log) to compare.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)} and \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}.\\
<\texttt{FILE\_2}>&A queriable stochastic language (model) to compare.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)} and \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}.\\

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi conformance jensen-shannon}}
\label{command:Ebi conformance jensen-shannon}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi conf jssc}.\\
Compute Jensen-Shannon stochastic conformance, which is 1 - the Jensen-Shannon distance.\\
More information: Compute Jensen-Shannon stochastic conformance is computed as follows:
        
        \begin{definition}[Jensen-Shannon stochastic conformance~\cite{DBLP:conf/icpm/LiLP24}]
            \label{def:jssc}
                Let $L$ be a finite stochastic language, let $M$ be a queriable stochastic langauge, and let $\Sigma$ be their combined alphabet.
                Then, the \emph{Jensen-Shannon stochastic conformance ($\text{jssc}$) of $L$ and $M$} is defined as follows: 
                \begin{align*}
                    \text{jssc}(L, M) ={}& 1 - \sqrt{\frac{\sum_{\sigma \in \Sigma^*} L(\sigma) \log_2 \frac{2 L(\sigma)}{L(\sigma) + M(\sigma)} + M(\sigma) \log_2 \frac{2M(\sigma)}{L(\sigma)+ M(\sigma)}}{2}}
                \end{align*}       
            \end{definition}~\\
\noindent Output: rootlog, which can be written as a rootlog.\\
\\This command is available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function conformance\_jensen\_shannon.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE\_1}>&A finite stochastic language to compare.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)} and \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}.\\
<\texttt{FILE\_2}>&A queriable stochastic language to compare.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi conformance markovian}}
\label{command:Ebi conformance markovian}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi conf ma}.\\
Compute the conformance between two stochastic languages using a stochastic Markovian abstraction, which represents languages based on the expected frequency of subtraces to handle partially matching traces.\\
More information: \cite{DBLP:journals/procsci/RochaLA25}~\\
\noindent Output: fraction, which can be written as a fraction.\\
\\This command is available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function conformance\_markovian.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE\_1}>&A finite stochastic language or a stochastic labelled Petri net (log) to compare. A model must be livelock-free and bounded.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)} and \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}.\\
<\texttt{FILE\_2}>&A finite stochastic language or a stochastic labelled Petri net (model) to compare. A model must be livelock-free and bounded.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)} and \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}.\\

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi conformance unit-earth-movers}}
\label{command:Ebi conformance unit-earth-movers}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi conf uemsc}.\\
Compute unit-earth movers' stochastic conformance, which is 1 - the total variation distance.\\
More information: Unit earth movers' stochastic conformance is computed as follows:
        
        \begin{definition}[Unit earth movers' stochastic conformance~\cite{DBLP:conf/bpm/LeemansSA19}]
            \label{def:uemsc}
                Let $L$ be a finite stochastic language and let $M$ be a queriable stochastic langauge.
                Then, the \emph{unit earth movers' stochastic conformance ($\text{uemsc}$) of $L$ and $M$} is defined as follows: 
                \begin{align*}
                    \text{uemsc}(L, M) ={}& 1 - \sum_{t \in L} \max(L(t) - M(t),0)
                \end{align*}       
            \end{definition}~\\
\noindent Output: fraction, which can be written as a fraction.\\
\\This command is available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function conformance\_unit\_earth\_movers.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE\_1}>&A finite stochastic language (log) to compare.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)} and \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}.\\
<\texttt{FILE\_2}>&A queriable stochastic language (model) to compare.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi conformance-non-stochastic alignments}}
\label{command:Ebi conformance-non-stochastic alignments}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi confns ali}.\\
Compute alignments.
The model must be able to terminate and its states must be bounded. The search performed is not optimised. For Petri nets, the ProM implementation may be more efficient.\\
More information: Alignments are computed according to the method described by Adriansyah~\cite{DBLP:conf/edoc/AdriansyahDA11}. 
        By default, all traces are computed concurrently on all CPU cores. 
        If this requires too much RAM, please see speed trick~\ref{speedtrick:multithreaded} in Section~\ref{sec:speedtricks} for how to reduce the number of CPU cores utilised.~\\
\noindent Output: stochastic language of alignments, which can be written as a language of alignments (.ali -- \cref{filehandler:language of alignments}) or a stochastic language of alignments (.sali -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic language of alignments}).\\
\\This command is not available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function conformance\_non\_stochastic\_alignments.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{FILE\_1}>&The finite stochastic language.\\

\clearpage\section{\texttt{Ebi conformance-non-stochastic escaping-edges-precision}}
\label{command:Ebi conformance-non-stochastic escaping-edges-precision}
Alias: \texttt{Ebi confns eep}.\\
Computes a prefix automaton of the alignments, where the states represent model states that have been visited by the alignments. The states are weighed by the probability of traces visiting them. The precision is then the sum of the number of taken edges out of each state multiplied by the weight of the state, divided by the sum of the number of outgoing edges multiplied by the weight of the states.\\
More information: Compute escaping-edges precision using the method of Adriansyah and Munoz-Gama~\cite{DBLP:conf/bpm/AdriansyahMCDA12}~\\
\noindent Output: fraction, which can be written as a fraction.\\
\\This command is not available in Java and ProM.
\\This command is available in the ebi-pm Python package using the function conformance\_non\_stochastic\_escaping\_edges\_precision.
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{lX}
\toprule
Parameter \\\midrule
<\texttt{ALIGNMENTS}>&The alignments.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic language of alignments]{stochastic language of alignments (.sali)}.\\
<\texttt{MODEL}>&The model from which the alignments were computed.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic business process model and notation]{stochastic business process model and notation (.sbpmn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows model]{directly follows model (.dfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:business process model and notation]{business process model and notation (.bpmn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite language]{finite language (.lang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:LoLa Petri net]{LoLa Petri net (.lola)}, \hyperref[filehandler:process tree markup language]{process tree markup language (.ptml)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:deterministic finite automaton]{deterministic finite automaton (.dfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:process tree]{process tree (.ptree)}, &\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\

&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input1\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header.

<\texttt{FILE}>&Any file supported by Ebi that can be converted.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad yes, though it can be given on STDIN by giving a `-' on the command line.\\
&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite language]{finite language (.lang)} and \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}.\\
\texttt{-t} or \texttt{--output\_type} <\texttt{OUTPUT\_TYPE}> &
The output file extension (without period). The default is lang. Possible values are dfa and lang.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The file to which the results must be written. Based on the file extension, Ebi will output either a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}) or a finite language (.lang -- \cref{filehandler:finite language}).
If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT as a finite language (.lang).\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-a} or \texttt{--approximate} & Use approximate arithmetic instead of exact arithmetic.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_activity} & The name or number of the column that contains the activity.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `1'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header. 
    With this flag set, the first line is read as an event. In the latter case, the columns are given numbers, starting from 0.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. \\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_quote\_character} & The character(s) that begin and end a quote.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `"'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_separator} & The character(s) that separate the columns.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `,'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_trace\_id} & The name or number of the column that contains the trace id.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_ocel\_case\_object\_type} & The object type that defines what a trace is. May be given as the name of the object type (which takes preference) or as the index of the object-type in the order of declaration.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_xes\_event\_classifier} & The attribute that defines, for each event, what its activity is.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `concept:name'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}

<\texttt{FILE}>&Any file supported by Ebi that can be converted. An SLPN must be livelock-free and bounded.\\

&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic process tree]{stochastic process tree (.sptree)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net]{stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic directly follows model]{stochastic directly follows model (.sdfm)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic deterministic finite automaton (.sdfa)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:directly follows graph]{directly follows graph (.dfg)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton]{stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa)} and \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}.\\
\texttt{-t} or \texttt{--output\_type} <\texttt{OUTPUT\_TYPE}> &
The output file extension (without period). The default is snfa. Possible values are dfa, bpmn, lpn, lola, pnml, slpn, pdf, svg, pdf, png and snfa.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The file to which the results must be written. Based on the file extension, Ebi will output either a deterministic finite automaton (.dfa -- \cref{filehandler:deterministic finite automaton}), a business process model and notation (.bpmn -- \cref{filehandler:business process model and notation}), a labelled Petri net (.lpn -- \cref{filehandler:labelled Petri net}), a LoLa Petri net (.lola -- \cref{filehandler:LoLa Petri net}), a Petri net markup language (.pnml -- \cref{filehandler:Petri net markup language}), a stochastic labelled Petri net (.slpn -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic labelled Petri net}), a portable document format (.pdf -- \cref{filehandler:portable document format}), a scalable vector graphics (.svg -- \cref{filehandler:scalable vector graphics}), a portable document format (.pdf -- \cref{filehandler:portable document format}), a portable network graphics (.png -- \cref{filehandler:portable network graphics}) or a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa -- \cref{filehandler:stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton}).
If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT as a stochastic non-deterministic finite automaton (.snfa).\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-a} or \texttt{--approximate} & Use approximate arithmetic instead of exact arithmetic.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_activity} & The name or number of the column that contains the activity.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `1'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header. 
    With this flag set, the first line is read as an event. In the latter case, the columns are given numbers, starting from 0.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. \\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_quote\_character} & The character(s) that begin and end a quote.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `"'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_separator} & The character(s) that separate the columns.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `,'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_trace\_id} & The name or number of the column that contains the trace id.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_ocel\_case\_object\_type} & The object type that defines what a trace is. May be given as the name of the object type (which takes preference) or as the index of the object-type in the order of declaration.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_xes\_event\_classifier} & The attribute that defines, for each event, what its activity is.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input FILE. The default value is `concept:name'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}

&\textit{Accepted values:}\quad \hyperref[filehandler:finite language]{finite language (.lang)}, \hyperref[filehandler:Python event log]{Python event log (.pel)}, \hyperref[filehandler:comma-separated values]{comma-separated values (.csv)}, \hyperref[filehandler:compressed event log]{compressed event log (.xes.gz)}, \hyperref[filehandler:extensible event stream]{extensible event stream (.xes)}, \hyperref[filehandler:finite stochastic language]{finite stochastic language (.slang)} and \hyperref[filehandler:object-centric event log]{object-centric event log (.ocel)}.\\
\texttt{-t} or \texttt{--output\_type} <\texttt{OUTPUT\_TYPE}> &
The output file extension (without period). The default is ptree. Possible values are bpmn, lpn, lola, pnml, ptree, ptml, pdf, svg, pdf and png.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-o} or \texttt{--output} <\texttt{FILE}> &
The file to which the results must be written. Based on the file extension, Ebi will output either a business process model and notation (.bpmn -- \cref{filehandler:business process model and notation}), a labelled Petri net (.lpn -- \cref{filehandler:labelled Petri net}), a LoLa Petri net (.lola -- \cref{filehandler:LoLa Petri net}), a Petri net markup language (.pnml -- \cref{filehandler:Petri net markup language}), a process tree (.ptree -- \cref{filehandler:process tree}), a process tree markup language (.ptml -- \cref{filehandler:process tree markup language}), a portable document format (.pdf -- \cref{filehandler:portable document format}), a scalable vector graphics (.svg -- \cref{filehandler:scalable vector graphics}), a portable document format (.pdf -- \cref{filehandler:portable document format}) or a portable network graphics (.png -- \cref{filehandler:portable network graphics}).
If the parameter is not given, the results will be written to STDOUT as a process tree (.ptree).\\
&\textit{Mandatory:} \quad no\\
\texttt{-a} or \texttt{--approximate} & Use approximate arithmetic instead of exact arithmetic.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_activity} & The name or number of the column that contains the activity.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input LANG. The default value is `1'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_no\_header} & Without this flag set, the first line is read as the header. 
    With this flag set, the first line is read as an event. In the latter case, the columns are given numbers, starting from 0.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input LANG. \\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_quote\_character} & The character(s) that begin and end a quote.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input LANG. The default value is `"'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_separator} & The character(s) that separate the columns.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input LANG. The default value is `,'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_csv\_trace\_id} & The name or number of the column that contains the trace id.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input LANG. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_ocel\_case\_object\_type} & The object type that defines what a trace is. May be given as the name of the object type (which takes preference) or as the index of the object-type in the order of declaration.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input LANG. The default value is `0'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\texttt{--input0\_xes\_event\_classifier} & The attribute that defines, for each event, what its activity is.. This parameter applies to some of the importers of the first input LANG. The default value is `concept:name'.\\
&\textit{Mandatory:}\quad no\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
A \textdagger{} indicates that the file type is converted on import, but that this conversion may fail.
